My gifted kid level of reading comprehension came in handy when I tried to make sense out of research papers I had not been trained to read.


08:50:30-08:50:41
I had translatable skills for my acting career, how to sit relatively still, allow myself to be vulnerable, cope with critical feedback, and collaborate with other creatives.


08:50:41-08:50:47
As a server, I had a flexible schedule, and I could get someone to cover for me when I was behind and needed to write.


08:50:47-08:50:51
I also had a notepad with me constantly while I worked, and often scribbled new ideas down.


08:50:51-08:50:58
I used to be frustrated by my inability to describe simple things in beautiful and complex ways like poets can.


08:50:58-08:51:03
I could only manage to describe even the most complex ideas in simple words.


08:51:03-08:51:07
Turns out, this is a talent that's useful in science communication.


08:51:07-08:51:14
It's a strength to be able to boil down complex information into words anyone can understand without losing too much of the nuance.


08:51:14-08:51:24
I began realizing many of my past successes happened when I'd been allowed to lean into my strengths, the pizzas I'd earned by reading books.


08:51:24-08:51:26
Thanks, Bookit.


08:51:26-08:51:28
The little tickets I'd drawn in CorelDRAW.


08:51:28-08:51:36
Getting that A in statistics class because my professor was cool with me taking it with my friend I wanted to impress, even though I hadn't registered for it yet.


08:51:36-08:51:41
The papers I'd written beautifully because I was able to choose a topic I was excited about.


08:51:41-08:51:49
I was amazing at waiting tables, and that benefited the restaurant that was more forgiving about me showing up four minutes late.


08:51:49-08:51:52
I got us many great reviews and glowing praise calls to corporate.


08:51:54-08:52:04
I remembered something I'd learned years into my YouTube career, when I'd been confused about how I'd become successful, even as I still struggled so much with so many things.


08:52:04-08:52:10
When I felt guilty about hiring a housekeeper instead of trying to be better at cleaning, I'd remind myself of this fact.


08:52:10-08:52:15
The most successful people aren't the ones who get good at what they're bad at.


08:52:15-08:52:17
They're the ones who lean into their strengths.


08:52:17-08:52:23
It's something we already and instinctively do when we're given the chance.


08:52:23-08:52:30
We can do it not just in terms of learning how to do things that are hard for us, but in doing more of what we're already good at and leveling up.


08:52:30-08:52:41
We have innate strengths, skills, talents, and aptitudes that others simply don't, and in combinations that you can't find anywhere else.


08:52:41-08:52:47
Because our brains work differently, we both already have and develop strengths others don't.


08:52:47-08:52:52
We think to use our skills and strengths in ways others don't.


08:52:52-08:53:01
Understanding this helped me understand why, despite everything I've learned about how impairing ADHD can be, I still like being neurodivergent.


08:53:01-08:53:06
As the world is beginning to acknowledge, interesting things happen in the margins.


08:53:06-08:53:13
Because of my neurodivergence, I've accomplished things others can't or maybe wouldn't even think to try.


08:53:13-08:53:16
And I did that by leaning into my strengths.


08:53:16-08:53:22
This isn't to say we have to be exceptional or do amazing things to make up for the fact that we have ADHD.


08:53:22-08:53:29
This belief stems from internalized ableism, same as the belief that we should be neurotypical.


08:53:29-08:53:35
It can be just as harmful or worse, because now we're expected to meet all the same standards and outperform too.


08:53:35-08:53:45
Like Dr. Halliwell, I want to highlight our strengths because otherwise, we might live our whole lives focused on what we're not good at.


08:53:45-08:53:51
We might not understand that we have strengths at all, because they're not where society expects them to be.


08:53:51-08:53:56
And because they often exist on the flip side of the traits we've been told are just bad.


08:53:56-08:54:07
We all need to get curious about what our different strengths are, and to learn how to lean on each other effectively, interdependently for strengths we ourselves do not have.


08:54:07-08:54:13
This is especially essential for those whose strengths and weaknesses are both far from average, who exist in the margins.


08:54:15-08:54:23
To use another D&D metaphor, you don't put a mage on the front lines, or expect them to battle a dragon single-handedly.


08:54:23-08:54:24
They'll die.


08:54:24-08:54:31
You put together a party of people with different skills and strengths so that everyone can focus on doing what they excel at.


08:54:31-08:54:33
No one can be good at all the things.


08:54:33-08:54:35
This is why you never split the party.


08:54:35-08:54:43
Sometimes we need to level up stats that hold us back, but it doesn't make sense to do that at the expense of what we're already good at.


08:54:44-08:54:51
I'm glad I didn't know this when I started out, because I definitely wouldn't have spent seven years focused on overcoming my challenges.


08:54:51-08:54:55
And I'm glad I learned all the tools I shared in this audiobook.


08:54:55-08:54:58
We can't totally opt out of the stuff we struggle with.


08:54:58-08:55:08
Some of these things are important for living a fulfilling life, like sleep, or making friends, or knowing roughly how time works.


08:55:08-08:55:15
It's nice to have tools for when I have to do things that aren't ADHD friendly, like the two-year-long project of publishing a book.


08:55:15-08:55:19
I needed to use many of these tools in the process.


08:55:19-08:55:26
Having tools and understanding how and when to use them can make it easier to apply our strengths in unique and world-changing ways.


08:55:26-08:55:32
But I want alternate universe me and you, the listener, to know this.


08:55:32-08:55:37
You don't have to wait until you're good at using these tools to work with your brain, not against it.


08:55:37-08:55:40
It's okay if you forget about strategies and can't find them again.


08:55:41-08:55:48
Working with your brain is something you can start doing right now, even if you don't remember a word of what you've heard in this book.


08:55:48-08:55:51
You can do it by leaning into your strengths.


08:55:51-08:55:55
It's not the complete picture of working with your brain, not against it.


08:55:55-08:56:00
But as I am humbled to realize, it is absolutely the most important part.


08:56:00-08:56:07
Our strengths are where our potential lies, and it makes the most sense to level them up.


08:56:07-08:56:11
Also, learning to navigate our challenges effectively takes time.


08:56:11-08:56:14
Leaning into your strengths can start right now.


08:56:14-08:56:21
You might, like me, not realize what your strengths are at first, because to you, it just feels like the stuff that isn't as hard.


08:56:21-08:56:25
It's easy to discount our strengths with, well, but that's just easy, right?


08:56:25-08:56:28
It's not, not for everyone.


08:56:28-08:56:32
You can start leaning into your strengths by doing the stuff that comes easily to you.


08:56:32-08:56:35
What do I find easy or effortless?


08:56:35-08:56:37
What do people say they admire that I can do?


08:56:37-08:56:39
What do people turn to me for help with?


08:56:40-08:56:46
When given the opportunity to do it however I want, how do I approach a hard task?


08:56:46-08:56:47
Those are your strengths.


08:56:47-08:56:51
We all have some, which brings me finally to this.


08:56:51-08:56:56
One of this community's greatest strengths is one another.


08:56:56-08:56:59
We all have different strengths.


08:56:59-08:57:03
It's often easier for us to do something for someone else than it is for ourselves.


08:57:03-08:57:04
We love to help.


08:57:04-08:57:06
We know what it's like to struggle.


08:57:07-08:57:14
This community is amazing at effective interdependence, and they have taught me that leaning into each other's strengths is a strength.


08:57:14-08:57:20
When we're able to do that, it's okay if we're weird.


08:57:20-08:57:22
It's okay if we struggle.


08:57:22-08:57:24
We do not need to try harder.


08:57:24-08:57:28
We are not a broken version of normal.


08:57:28-08:57:30
We are different.


08:57:30-08:57:31
We are beautiful.


08:57:31-08:57:32
And we are not alone.


08:57:37-08:57:38
>> This is Jessica McCabe.
